<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every sufficiently large fixed <i>k</i>, deciding whether two <i>n</i>-vertex graphs are <i>k</i>-Weisfeiler–Leman equivalent requires $`n^{\Omega(k)}`$ deterministic sequential time in the worst case. The bound holds at every sufficiently large graph order, even for simple connected uncolored graphs of diameter at most two, without a complexity assumption. Inputs are explicit adjacency matrices; the models are multitape Turing machines and sequential logarithmic-word RAMs with fixed polynomial-bit-time instructions. Both joint and separate replacement conventions are covered.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $k_0$ with the following property. For every fixed integer $k\ge k_0$ and every correct deterministic decider $A$ for $k$-Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence, there is an integer $n_0=n_0(A,k)$ such that $$T_A(n)\ge n^{ck} \qquad\text{for every integer }n\ge n_0.$$ This holds for multitape Turing machines and for sequential random-access machines with $O(\log(n+2))$-bit addresses and words, boundedly many memory accesses per instruction, and fixed instructions computable in time polynomial in the word length. The same bound holds if $A$ is required to decide correctly only on pairs of simple connected uncolored graphs of diameter at most two, with $T_A(n)$ maximized over that class. It applies both to the joint replacement-multiset convention for $k$-dimensional refinement and to the separate-multiset convention defined below.
  - Explanation: Every correct deterministic sequential equivalence decider eventually has a worst-case exponent growing with the fixed dimension. The threshold may depend on the algorithm and dimension; the exponent constant is absolute.
- **C2** (classification, [source](#stmt-game-wl)): For the joint convention put $K=k+1$, and for the separate convention put $K=k$. For graphs of equal positive order the following hold.* 1. *Two $k$-tuples have the same stable color if and only if Duplicator has a winning $K$-pair strategy from the position marking those tuples on $k$ corresponding pairs.* 2. *The graphs have equal stable $k$-tuple color histograms if and only if Duplicator has a winning $K$-pair strategy from the empty position.
  - Explanation: Stable tuple colors and stable histograms are characterized exactly by winning bijective-game positions, with the parameter determined by the replacement convention.
- **C3** (classification, [source](#stmt-game-union)): Suppose $k\geq3$ and $G,H$ are connected graphs of diameter at most two. Run either convention on $D=G\sqcup H$, including mixed tuples. The stable color histograms restricted to the pure tuples $V(G)^k$ and $V(H)^k$ are equal if and only if the separate-input histograms from Definition <a href="#game:conventions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="game:conventions">2</a> are equal.
  - Explanation: For the stated connected graph class, comparing pure tuples in the disjoint union agrees with comparing the input graphs separately.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-comp-reduction)): For fixed $r$ and fixed description shape, an instance as above determines two equally large simple uncolored connected graphs $G^0,G^1$, of diameter at most two and order $$O_{r,\mathrm{shape}}(m^6),$$ such that Duplicator wins the $K$-pair bijective game on $G^0,G^1$ if and only if the instance has individual-edge arc consistency. The construction uses the bounded scalar-form data together with the one-coordinate tables, and enumerates $O_{r,\mathrm{shape}}(m^2)$ blocks without enumerating all full addresses. An arbitrary equal number of additional vertices can be added to the two graphs while preserving the equivalence, connectedness, and diameter bound.
  - Explanation: A fixed short consistency description yields equally large uncolored graphs whose bijective-game equivalence is exactly local scalar-image consistency. Equal padding preserves the result.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-necessity)): A block-preserving winning strategy with $r+1$ pairs implies individual-edge arc consistency.
  - Explanation: Queries of homogeneous zero valuations transport endpoint scalar images and recover individual-edge consistency from a winning block-preserving strategy.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-support)): If $\|U\|\leq r+1$, every component’s type support is a tree containing at most one real type. With no real type it lies in the interior of one wire; with a real type it consists of that type and short arms of incident wires.
  - Explanation: A small block collection cannot traverse an entire subdivided wire, so each component has tree support near at most one real type.
- **C7** (classification, [source](#stmt-comp-wrapping)): For block sets contained in a set $U$ of size at most $r+1$:* 1. *Every wrapping collection has at least $r$ blocks.* 2. *A wrapping collection of size $r$ has one block in every row, all in one component, and determines a consistent full address in the aligned coordinates of that component’s type support.* 3. *All wrapping subcollections of $U$ of size $r$ lie in one component and determine the same aligned full address.
  - Explanation: Wrapping reveals a full address only after enough rows are present; minimal wrapping subcollections in the permitted block set recover the same address.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-projection)): For $V\subseteq U$ with $\|U\|\leq r+1$, $$\label{comp:projection-equation} \mathcal H_U\big\|_V=\mathcal H_V.$$
  - Explanation: Compatible offset families restrict exactly. Every retained readout assignment extends, with changes of nonwrapping shifts absorbed into corner potentials.
- **C9** (construction, [source](#stmt-comp-sufficiency)): Individual-edge arc consistency implies a block-preserving winning strategy with $r+1$ pairs on the valuation graphs.
  - Explanation: Translations by compatible offsets provide block bijections preserving the marked partial isomorphism under every continuation.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-circ-simulation)): To such a circuit one can associate an individual-edge arc-consistency instance over $\mathbb{F}_2$, with vector dimensions at most two, that has nonempty arc-consistent sets if and only if every test gate is false.* *If the circuit is specified by gate types indexed by $[m]^r$, connection schemas with product domains and componentwise partial bijections, and seed and test requests that are unions of product domains, then the consistency instance has the same form. A bounded number of gate types, connection schemas, and product requests gives a bounded number of consistency types and edge schemas, independently of $m$.
  - Explanation: Private scalar-image buffers encode directed monotone circuit propagation. Consistency exists exactly when all terminal tests are false.
- **C11** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-circ-construction)): Fix $r\geq3$ and a short consistency template with bounded numbers of types and schemas and bounded vector dimensions. Suppose its one-coordinate domains and maps are supplied by a bounded number of tables, each with $O(m)$ entries in $[m]$. The two uncolored graphs of Theorem <a href="#comp:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="comp:reduction">8</a> have a common vertex count $$N=O(m^6+1),$$ where the constant depends only on the fixed template and $r$. They may be padded to any common order $n\geq N$ without changing the equivalence conclusion of that theorem.* *For $n\geq\max\{N,m^{20}\}$, their adjacency matrices can be constructed from the tables in $O(n^4)$ multitape Turing-machine time. The exponent is absolute; the multiplicative constant may depend on the template and $r$. If the tables can themselves be constructed from a word of length $m$ in $O(m^3)$ time, this preprocessing is included in the same bound.
  - Explanation: The short coordinate tables support explicit enumeration and adjacency-matrix construction at any permitted padded order, without enumerating all full addresses.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-circ-computation)): Fix such a machine $M$ and an integer $r\geq3$, and put $d=\lfloor r/3\rfloor$. There is a single effective construction, depending only on $M$ and $r$, with the following property. For each $m\geq2$ for which $M$ halts on every length-$m$ input within an integer bound $T_0(m)$ satisfying $$\label{circ:time-space-slack} T_0(m)<m^d-1, \qquad 2T_0(m)+m+2<m^{r-d},$$ the construction takes $w\in\{0,1\}^m$ to two simple uncolored graphs of common order $O_{r,M}(m^6)$ such that $M$ accepts $w$ if and only if the two graphs are inequivalent in the $(r+1)$-pebble bijective game. Thus they are inequivalent for joint-update $r$-dimensional $\mathrm{WL}$ precisely when $M$ accepts $w$.* *The consistency description used in the construction has $O_{r,M}(1)$ types and edge schemas, dimensions at most two, and $O_{r,M}(1)$ one-coordinate tables with $O(m)$ entries each. These tables can be constructed from $w$ in $O_{r,M}(m^3)$ time without running $M$ on $w$. For every sufficiently large $m$ satisfying <a href="#circ:time-space-slack" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="circ:time-space-slack">[circ:time-space-slack]</a>, the graphs can be padded to any common order $n\geq m^{20}$ and their adjacency matrices constructed in $O_{r,M}(n^4)$ multitape time. In particular, for $m^{20}\leq n<(m+1)^{20}$ the construction time is a polynomial in $m$ of absolute degree, independently of $r$ and $M$.
  - Explanation: Under the stated time and spatial slack, a product-indexed local circuit records the machine computation. Acceptance has inequivalence polarity in the resulting graph pair.
- **C13** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-diag-simulation)): For every fixed deterministic multitape machine $A$ there is a fixed deterministic one-tape machine $\widehat A$ with the same decisions such that, if $A$ halts after $T$ steps on an input of length $m$, then $\widehat A$ halts within $$O_A\bigl((m+T+1)^2\bigr)$$ steps on that input.
  - Explanation: Marked tracks and repeated sweeps simulate a fixed multitape machine on a fixed one-tape machine with the stated quadratic overhead.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-diag-language)): For every integer $s\geq 1$ there is a binary language $H_s$ with the following properties.* 1. *There is a fixed deterministic one-tape decider $M_s$ and a constant $C_s$ such that, on every input of length $m\geq 0$, its running time is at most $$C_s(m+m^s+2)^{20}.$$* 2. *If $D$ is any correct deterministic one-tape decider for $H_s$, then, for every sufficiently large integer $m$, the worst-case running time of $D$ on inputs of length $m$ is strictly greater than $m^s$.* 3. *After their decisions have been recorded, the accepting and rejecting configurations of $M_s$ may be kept fixed forever. This gives a stationary extension of its computation suitable for the local update representation.
  - Explanation: Padded machine designations give a diagonal language with an explicit decider and a worst-case lower bound at every sufficiently large input length.
- **C15** (construction, [source](#stmt-trans-instances)): For every sufficiently large fixed $r$, there is an integer $m_0(r)$ with the following property. Given a word $w$ of length $m\ge m_0(r)$ and any $n\in I_m$, one can construct two simple uncolored $n$-vertex graphs $G_{w,n}$ and $G'_{w,n}$ such that $$\label{trans:polarity} w\in H_s \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad G_{w,n}\not\equiv_{k\text{-}\mathrm{WL}}G'_{w,n}.$$ Both graphs are connected and have diameter at most two. Their explicit adjacency matrices can be constructed in $O_r(n^4)$ multitape time.
  - Explanation: The diagonal language is transferred to connected uncolored graph pairs at every prescribed order in the padding interval, with membership corresponding to inequivalence.
- **C16** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-trans-cutoff)): There is an absolute constant $D$ with the following property. Fix a sufficiently large $r$, with $s,p$ as in <a href="#trans:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:parameters">[trans:parameters]</a>, and a correct deterministic equivalence decider $A$ in either stated model. There is a correct one-tape decider $B$ for $H_s$ that, on every sufficiently long word $w$ of length $m$, first tries the pairs of Lemma <a href="#trans:instances" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="trans:instances">20</a> for all $n\in I_m$, allowing at most $m^p$ steps of $A$ on each pair. It returns the first completed answer, with reversed polarity, or falls back to $M_s$ if no trial completes. The one-tape time used before fallback, or before returning a completed answer, is at most $$\label{trans:cutoff-cost} O_{A,r}\bigl(m^{D+50p}\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: A fixed cutoff decider tries every padded order in the interval, reverses the first completed answer, and otherwise uses the diagonal-language decider.

It applies both to the joint replacement-multiset convention for $k$-dimensional refinement and to the separate-multiset convention defined below.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-133.3"

        "anchor": "stmt-comp-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $r$ and fixed description shape, an instance as above determines two equally large simple uncolored connected graphs $G^0,G^1$, of diameter at most two and order $$O_{r,\\mathrm{shape}}(m^6),$$ such that Duplicator wins the $K$-pair bijective game on $G^0,G^1$ if and only if the instance has individual-edge arc consistency.           "exact_quote": "To such a circuit one can associate an individual-edge arc-consistency instance over $\\mathbb{F}_2$, with vector dimensions at most two, that has nonempty arc-consistent sets if and only if every test gate is false.*\n\n*If the circuit is specified by gate types indexed by $[m]^r$, connection schemas with product domains and componentwise partial bijections, and seed and test requests that are unions of product domains, then the consistency instance has the same form. A bounded number of gate types, connection schemas, and product requests gives a bounded number of consistency types and edge schemas, independently of $m$.",
          "source_id": "PM-133.3"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-circ-simulation"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-circ-construction",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $r\\geq3$ and a short consistency template with bounded numbers of types and schemas and bounded vector dimensions. Suppose its one-coordinate domains and maps are supplied by a bounded number of tables, each with $O(m)$ entries in $[m]$. The two uncolored graphs of Theorem <a href=\"#comp:reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:reduction\">8</a> have a common vertex count $$N=O(m^6+1),$$ where the constant depends only on the fixed template and $r$. They may be padded to any common order $n\\geq N$ without changing the equivalence conclusion of that theorem.*\n\n*For $n\\geq\\max\\{N,m^{20}\\}$, their adjacency matrices can be constructed from the tables in $O(n^4)$ multitape Turing-machine time. The exponent is absolute; the multiplicative constant may depend on the template and $r$. If the tables can themselves be constructed from a word of length $m$ in $O(m^3)$ time, this preprocessing is included in the same bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-133.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-circ-computation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix such a machine $M$ and an integer $r\\geq3$, and put $d=\\lfloor r/3\\rfloor$. There is a single effective construction, depending only on $M$ and $r$, with the following property. For each $m\\geq2$ for which $M$ halts on every length-$m$ input within an integer bound $T_0(m)$ satisfying $$\\label{circ:time-space-slack}\n T_0(m)<m^d-1,\n \\qquad\n 2T_0(m)+m+2<m^{r-d},$$ the construction takes $w\\in\\{0,1\\}^m$ to two simple uncolored graphs of common order $O_{r,M}(m^6)$ such that $M$ accepts $w$ if and only if the two graphs are inequivalent in the $(r+1)$-pebble bijective game. Thus they are inequivalent for joint-update $r$-dimensional $\\mathrm{WL}$ precisely when $M$ accepts $w$.*\n\n*The consistency description used in the construction has $O_{r,M}(1)$ types and edge schemas, dimensions at most two, and $O_{r,M}(1)$ one-coordinate tables with $O(m)$ entries each. These tables can be constructed from $w$ in $O_{r,M}(m^3)$ time without running $M$ on $w$. For every sufficiently large $m$ satisfying <a href=\"#circ:time-space-slack\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"circ:time-space-slack\">[circ:time-space-slack]</a>, the graphs can be padded to any common order $n\\geq m^{20}$ and their adjacency matrices constructed in $O_{r,M}(n^4)$ multitape time. In particular, for $m^{20}\\leq n<(m+1)^{20}$ the construction time is a polynomial in $m$ of absolute degree, independently of $r$ and $M$.",
        "source_id": "PM-133.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-diag-simulation",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed deterministic multitape machine $A$ there is a fixed deterministic one-tape machine $\\widehat A$ with the same decisions such that, if $A$ halts after $T$ steps on an input of length $m$, then $\\widehat A$ halts within $$O_A\\bigl((m+T+1)^2\\bigr)$$ steps on that input.",
        "source_id": "PM-133.3"

          "source_id": "PM-133.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-diag-language",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $s\\geq 1$ there is a binary language $H_s$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *There is a fixed deterministic one-tape decider $M_s$ and a constant $C_s$ such that, on every input of length $m\\geq 0$, its running time is at most $$C_s(m+m^s+2)^{20}.$$*\n\n2.  *If $D$ is any correct deterministic one-tape decider for $H_s$, then, for every sufficiently large integer $m$, the worst-case running time of $D$ on inputs of length $m$ is strictly greater than $m^s$.*\n\n3.  *After their decisions have been recorded, the accepting and rejecting configurations of $M_s$ may be kept fixed forever. This gives a stationary extension of its computation suitable for the local update representation.",
        "source_id": "PM-133.3"
      },

This gives a stationary extension of its computation suitable for the local update representation.",

        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large fixed $r$, there is an integer $m_0(r)$ with the following property. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Joint and separate refinement conventions** ([source](#stmt-game-conventions)): Let $k\geq 2$. The initial color $\chi_0^X(\bar v)$ records the atomic type of $\bar v$ in $X$: all coordinate equalities, adjacencies, and vertex colors. The *joint* convention updates by $$\chi_{t+1}^X(\bar v)= \left(\chi_t^X(\bar v), \left\{\!\left\{ \bigl(\chi_t^X(\bar v[i\leftarrow z])\bigr)_{i=1}^k :z\in V(X) \right\}\!\right\}\right).$$ The *separate* convention instead records the ordered list $$\left( \left\{\!\left\{ \chi_t^X(\bar v[i\leftarrow z]):z\in V(X) \right\}\!\right\}\right)_{i=1}^k$$ along with the old color. In either convention, the same recursive color names are used for both input graphs. Two graphs are equivalent if their $k$-tuple color histograms agree at every round.
- **Bijective game** ([source](#stmt-game-definition)): The bijective game with $K$ pairs is played on $G,H$ as follows. Spoiler may remove any marked pairs and choose a vacant pair. Duplicator then announces a bijection $f:V(G)\to V(H)$, after which Spoiler chooses $z\in V(G)$ and marks $z,f(z)$ with that pair. Duplicator loses as soon as the marked correspondence fails to preserve equality, adjacency, or vertex colors. A winning strategy for Duplicator must survive every finite continuation, and hence every infinite play. If the graph orders differ, Duplicator cannot announce a bijection and loses.
- **Individual-edge arc consistency** ([source](#stmt-comp-ac)): The instance has *individual-edge arc consistency* if there are nonempty sets $Q_{P,\mathbf a}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_2^{d_P}$, for every real type and every address, such that $$\label{comp:ac-equation} \phi_{e,0}(Q_{P,\mathbf a}) =\phi_{e,1}(Q_{R,\boldsymbol\sigma_e(\mathbf a)}) \qquad(e,\ \mathbf a\in A_e).$$ Each equality is imposed separately. In particular, parallel comparisons are not combined into a single relation.
- **Wrapping block collection** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A collection of blocks is *wrapping* if its corner graph has a closed walk traversing an odd number of horizontal edges in row $r$, counted with multiplicity.
- **Address alphabet** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Put $[m]=\{0,\ldots,m-1\}$, where $m\geq 2$.
- **Description shape** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A *description shape* fixes the types, their dimensions, and the number and incidence of the schemas.
- **Absolute polynomial degree** ([source](#pm-h-5)): An *absolute polynomial degree* is independent of those fixed parameters, although its multiplicative constant and the starting size need not be.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Worst-case equivalence decision time | ≥ | $n^{ck}$ | Fixed sufficiently large dimension; eventual threshold depends on the program and dimension. |
| Q2 | Absolute exponent constant | = | $10^{-9}$ | Fixed sufficiently large dimension. |
| Q3 | Unpadded graph order | ≤ | $O_{r,\mathrm{shape}}(m^6)$ | Auxiliary parameter and description shape fixed; address alphabet grows. |
| Q4 | Enumerated block count | ≤ | $O_{r,\mathrm{shape}}(m^2)$ | Auxiliary parameter and description shape fixed. |
| Q5 | Private internal types per wire | = | $4(r+1)$ | Fixed auxiliary parameter at least the stated threshold. |
| Q6 | Marks per valuation vertex in its block | = | $j(N+1)$ | Common valuation-vertex count and block numbering. |
| Q7 | Padded matrix construction time | ≤ | $O(n^4)$ | Fixed template and auxiliary parameter. |
| Q8 | Time digit count | = | $\lfloor r/3\rfloor$ | Fixed machine and auxiliary parameter. |
| Q9 | One-tape simulation time | ≤ | $O_A\bigl((m+T+1)^2\bigr)$ | Input length and halting time vary; simulated machine fixed. |
| Q10 | Diagonal-language decider time | ≤ | $C_s(m+m^s+2)^{20}$ | Fixed positive diagonal exponent; input length varies. |
| Q11 | Any correct diagonal-language decider worst-case time | > | $m^s$ | Fixed positive diagonal exponent; starting length may depend on the decider. |
| Q12 | Diagonal exponent | = | $\left\lfloor\frac{r}{1000}\right\rfloor$ | Fixed sufficiently large auxiliary parameter. |
| Q13 | Cutoff exponent | = | $\left\lfloor\frac{s}{1000}\right\rfloor$ | Fixed sufficiently large diagonal exponent. |
| Q14 | Cutoff procedure time before fallback | ≤ | $O_{A,r}\bigl(m^{D+50p}\bigr)$ | Absolute simulation exponent; program and auxiliary parameter fixed. |

The construction uses the bounded scalar-form data together with the one-coordinate tables, and enumerates $O_{r,\\mathrm{shape}}(m^2)$ blocks without enumerating all full addresses. An arbitrary equal number of additional vertices can be added to the two graphs while preserving the equivalence, connectedness, and diameter bound.",
```json
            "source_id": "PM-133.3"
          }
        ],

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-circ-construction",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $r\\geq3$ and a short consistency template with bounded numbers of types and schemas and bounded vector dimensions. Suppose its one-coordinate domains and maps are supplied by a bounded number of tables, each with $O(m)$ entries in $[m]$. The two uncolored graphs of Theorem <a href=\"#comp:reduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"comp:reduction\">8</a> have a common vertex count $$N=O(m^6+1),$$ where the constant depends only on the fixed template and $r$. They may be padded to any common order $n\\geq N$ without changing the equivalence conclusion of that theorem.*\n\n*For $n\\geq\\max\\{N,m^{20}\\}$, their adjacency matrices can be constructed from the tables in $O(n^4)$ multitape Turing-machine time. The exponent is absolute; the multiplicative constant may depend on the template and $r$. If the tables can themselves be constructed from a word of length $m$ in $O(m^3)$ time, this preprocessing is included in the same bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-133.3"
        }
      ],

    {
      "computational_model": null,
      "effectivity": "effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-circ-computation",
          "exact_quote": "Fix such a machine $M$ and an integer $r\\geq3$, and put $d=\\lfloor r/3\\rfloor$. There is a single effective construction, depending only on $M$ and $r$, with the following property. For each $m\\geq2$ for which $M$ halts on every length-$m$ input within an integer bound $T_0(m)$ satisfying $$\\label{circ:time-space-slack}\n T_0(m)<m^d-1,\n \\qquad\n 2T_0(m)+m+2<m^{r-d},$$ the construction takes $w\\in\\{0,1\\}^m$ to two simple uncolored graphs of common order $O_{r,M}(m^6)$ such that $M$ accepts $w$ if and only if the two graphs are inequivalent in the $(r+1)$-pebble bijective game. Thus they are inequivalent for joint-update $r$-dimensional $\\mathrm{WL}$ precisely when $M$ accepts $w$.*\n\n*The consistency description used in the construction has $O_{r,M}(1)$ types and edge schemas, dimensions at most two, and $O_{r,M}(1)$ one-coordinate tables with $O(m)$ entries each. These tables can be constructed from $w$ in $O_{r,M}(m^3)$ time without running $M$ on $w$. For every sufficiently large $m$ satisfying <a href=\"#circ:time-space-slack\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"circ:time-space-slack\">[circ:time-space-slack]</a>, the graphs can be padded to any common order $n\\geq m^{20}$ and their adjacency matrices constructed in $O_{r,M}(n^4)$ multitape time. In particular, for $m^{20}\\leq n<(m+1)^{20}$ the construction time is a polynomial in $m$ of absolute degree, independently of $r$ and $M$.",
          "source_id": "PM-133.3"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-diag-language",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $s\\geq 1$ there is a binary language $H_s$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *There is a fixed deterministic one-tape decider $M_s$ and a constant $C_s$ such that, on every input of length $m\\geq 0$, its running time is at most $$C_s(m+m^s+2)^{20}.$$*\n\n2.  *If $D$ is any correct deterministic one-tape decider for $H_s$, then, for every sufficiently large integer $m$, the worst-case running time of $D$ on inputs of length $m$ is strictly greater than $m^s$.*\n\n3.  *After their decisions have been recorded, the accepting and rejecting configurations of $M_s$ may be kept fixed forever.         }

          "anchor": "stmt-trans-cutoff",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $D$ with the following property. Fix a sufficiently large $r$, with $s,p$ as in <a href=\"#trans:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"trans:parameters\">[trans:parameters]</a>, and a correct deterministic equivalence decider $A$ in either stated model. There is a correct one-tape decider $B$ for $H_s$ that, on every sufficiently long word $w$ of length $m$, first tries the pairs of Lemma <a href=\"#trans:instances\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"trans:instances\">20</a> for all $n\\in I_m$, allowing at most $m^p$ steps of $A$ on each pair. It returns the first completed answer, with reversed polarity, or falls back to $M_s$ if no trial completes. The one-tape time used before fallback, or before returning a completed answer, is at most $$\\label{trans:cutoff-cost}\n  O_{A,r}\\bigl(m^{D+50p}\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-133.3"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
```

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-game-wl) / [proof](#proof-stmt-game-wl): Stable-color witness bijections and induction on refinement rounds. -> Exact game characterization.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-comp-reduction) / [proof](#pm-h-13): Subdivide schemas, compress adjacent address coordinates, and encode block colors by degrees. -> A padded uncolored graph pair encoding consistency.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-comp-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-projection): Recover wrapping addresses and absorb nonwrapping shift changes into corner potentials. -> Exact restriction and extension of offset families.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-comp-sufficiency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-sufficiency): Translate legal homogeneous valuations by compatible affine offsets. -> A block-preserving winning strategy.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-circ-simulation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-circ-simulation): Use private scalar-image buffers with separately imposed comparisons. -> Consistency exactly when every test is false.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-circ-computation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-circ-computation): Express the local update by monotone gates and product-domain carry and borrow schemas. -> Acceptance encoded by graph inequivalence.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-diag-language) / [proof](#proof-stmt-diag-language): Simulate the designated machine on the entire padded input up to the cutoff and reverse its acceptance. -> A language hard at every sufficiently large length.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-trans-instances) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-instances): Apply the computation reduction and pad equally to every prescribed order in the interval. -> Explicit hard graph pairs at every interval order.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-trans-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-cutoff): Try all interval orders under bounded simulation and fall back to the diagonal-language machine. -> A correct one-tape decider with an absolute bound on its trial prefix.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-cutoff-2): Contradict every-length diagonal hardness if any interval order is fast; cover all eventual graph orders. -> The eventual unconditional worst-case lower bound.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-buffer**: A false-source buffer retains full separate coordinate images while its sum image is the prescribed nonempty destination image; a true-source buffer has zero singleton coordinate and sum images.
- **I-terminal**: The circuit simulation requires terminal test gates; their false scalar image is not the full image used for ordinary false sources.
- **I-fixed**: The compressed graph construction fixes the address parameter and description shape as the address alphabet grows.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-joint**: Combine the buffer comparisons into a joint constraint. -> Common scalar-image support is replaced by simultaneous vector agreement, changing the consistency requirement.
- **A-terminal**: Use a test gate as a source in the circuit simulation. -> A false test has a singleton image and cannot serve as an ordinary false source with full output image.
- **A-uniform**: Treat the compressed construction as a polynomial compiler for an input address dimension. -> Fixed-parameter constants and description shapes do not give the claimed uniform compiler.
- **A-runtime**: Interpret the sequential lower bound as a hardware throughput prediction. -> An asymptotic bound for the specified sequential model does not supply measured latency or parallel performance.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The scalar-image buffer is a finite constructive component of the circuit simulation. Its local verification does not certify the complete circuit reduction or the asymptotic time lower bound.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with true_source Boolean and domain a nonempty sorted subset of binary scalars.
# PRECONDITIONS: A true source requires domain [0]; a false source permits [0], [1], or [0,1].
# OUTPUTS: Sorted maximal buffer subset having the required separate coordinate and sum images.
# TIME: Constant; enumerate the binary pairs.
# SPACE: Constant; store the buffer subset.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact fmpz arithmetic reduced modulo the binary field characteristic.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises ValueError; no approximate arithmetic is used.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz
request = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
flag, domain = request['true_source'], request['domain']
if type(flag) is not bool or domain not in ([0], [1], [0, 1]) or any(type(v) is not int for v in domain):
    raise ValueError('invalid binary domain')
if flag and domain != [0]:
    raise ValueError('true source requires zero destination image')
if flag:
    pairs = [[0, 0]]
else:
    pairs = [[a, b] for a in range(2) for b in range(2)
             if int((fmpz(a) + fmpz(b)) % 2) in domain]
print(json.dumps(pairs, separators=(',', ':')))
```

```json
        "mutation": "Delete the binary pair with both coordinates nonzero.",
        "observed_exit": 8,
        "receipt_sha256": "333455ac8c1d4eeddc76e2a13d70ad7850b441d753d58bb1f7d17122000c3114"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "The coordinate images cease to be the zero singleton.",
        "id": "M-truth",
        "mutation": "Replace the true-source buffer with the nonzero diagonal pair.",
        "observed_exit": 8,
        "receipt_sha256": "300368679764c6d9b1a6fffe997b0c19f14eb427fdeb7409987f55960baf0471"
      }
    ],
    "oracle_code": "import json, sys\nfrom itertools import combinations\nfrom pathlib import Path\ntry:\n    task = json.loads(Path(sys.argv[1]).read_text())\n    answer = json.loads(Path(sys.argv[2]).read_text())\n    promised = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n    bit_image = {0} if task['true_source'] else {0, 1}\n    universe = ((0,0), (0,1), (1,0), (1,1))\n    feasible = []\n    for cardinality in range(1, len(universe)+1):\n        for subset in combinations(universe, cardinality):\n            first, second = set(), set()\n            totals = set()\n            for left, right in subset:\n                first.add(left)\n                second.add(right)\n                totals.add(left ^ right)\n            if first == second == bit_image and totals == set(task['domain']):\n                feasible.append(set(subset))\n    maximal = [s for s in feasible if not any(s < t for t in feasible)]\n    if len(maximal) != 1:\n        sys.exit(5)\n    canonical = [list(pair) for pair in sorted(maximal[0])]\n    # Check the concrete witness directly, independently of the subset search.\n    if not answer or any(type(pair) is not list or len(pair) != 2 or\n                         any(type(bit) is not int or bit not in (0,1) for bit in pair) for pair in answer):\n        sys.exit(6)\n    rows = [tuple(pair) for pair in answer]\n    if len(set(rows)) != len(rows):\n        sys.exit(7)\n    if {p[0] for p in rows} != bit_image or {p[1] for p in rows} != bit_image:\n        sys.exit(8)\n    if {p[0] ^ p[1] for p in rows} != set(task['domain']):\n        sys.exit(9)\n    sys.exit(0 if answer == canonical == promised else 10)\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(11)\n",
    "output_contract": "The sorted nonempty buffer subset with separate coordinate images matching the source image and sum image matching the destination image.",
    "reason": "The scalar-image buffer is a finite constructive component of the circuit simulation. Its local verification does not certify the complete circuit reduction or the asymptotic time lower bound.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with true_source Boolean and domain a nonempty sorted subset of binary scalars.\n# PRECONDITIONS: A true source requires domain [0]; a false source permits [0], [1], or [0,1].\n# OUTPUTS: Sorted maximal buffer subset having the required separate coordinate and sum images.\n# TIME: Constant; enumerate the binary pairs.\n# SPACE: Constant; store the buffer subset.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact fmpz arithmetic reduced modulo the binary field characteristic.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises ValueError; no approximate arithmetic is used.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpz\nrequest = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nflag, domain = request['true_source'], request['domain']\nif type(flag) is not bool or domain not in ([0], [1], [0, 1]) or any(type(v) is not int for v in domain):\n    raise ValueError('invalid binary domain')\nif flag and domain != [0]:\n    raise ValueError('true source requires zero destination image')\nif flag:\n    pairs = [[0, 0]]\nelse:\n    pairs = [[a, b] for a in range(2) for b in range(2)\n             if int((fmpz(a) + fmpz(b)) % 2) in domain]\nprint(json.dumps(pairs, separators=(',', ':')))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "[[0,0],[1,1]]",
        "input": "{\"true_source\": false, \"domain\": [0]}"

        "expected": "[[0,1],[1,0]]",
        "input": "{\"true_source\": false, \"domain\": [1]}"
      },
      {

        "input": "{\"true_source\": false, \"domain\": [0, 1]}"
      },
      {
        "expected": "[[0,0]]",
        "input": "{\"true_source\": true, \"domain\": [0]}"
      }
    ]
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A service that decides fixed-dimension Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence can use the eventual worst-case lower bound to reject sublinear dependence of the exponent on the dimension, when its representation and computational model match the theorem. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | A simulator of the defined bijective game can implement the move order and partial-isomorphism loss condition; the connection to refinement depends on the replacement convention. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | A reduction-construction tool can generate explicit graph pairs from a fixed short consistency template using one-coordinate tables and padding. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The lower bound constrains every correct deterministic equivalence decider in the stated models, including algorithms that do not execute refinement. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The construction gives a concrete representation of product-address consistency using local blocks and one-coordinate tables, without listing full addresses. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An accelerator-specific construction, execution bound, or hardware measurement is ABSENT; the theorem supplies a deterministic sequential model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A cryptographic primitive or security contract is ABSENT; the result is an unconditional worst-case bound for graph-equivalence deciders. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A learning procedure, generalization guarantee, or training contract is ABSENT; the theorem concerns exact deterministic graph-equivalence decisions. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A finite-domain consistency component can adapt the separate scalar-image comparisons and private buffers when its task is the specified local-support condition; an optimization objective is ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A communication protocol or distributed execution guarantee is ABSENT; the computational models are sequential. |
| other (finite_model_theory) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Finite-model-theory experiments can use the exact relation between stable tuple colors and bijective-game strategies while preserving the convention-dependent number of pairs. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-model** (formal_scope): The lower bound concerns deterministic computation in the stated sequential machine models. Blocked use: ['Extending the result to randomized algorithms, nonuniform advice, parallel computation, or unit-cost operations on unbounded integers.']
- **L-input** (hypothesis): The decision problem uses explicit adjacency matrices and a dimension fixed independently of input size. Blocked use: ['Transferring the running-time exponent directly to succinct encodings or adjacency-list inputs.', 'Claiming a uniform compiler for an input dimension that grows with graph order.']
- **L-threshold** (edge_case): The eventual graph-order threshold may depend on both the decider and the fixed dimension. Blocked use: ['Treating the asymptotic lower bound as a measured latency prediction or a uniform finite-size performance threshold.']
- **L-compiler** (hypothesis): Compressed-construction constants depend on the fixed description shape and parameter. Blocked use: ['Claiming a polynomial compiler when the parameter grows with the input.']
- **L-degree** (counterexample_scope): The diameter promise does not provide a bounded-degree promise. Blocked use: ['Applying the restricted-class lower bound to bounded-degree graph families.']
- **L-local** (formal_scope): Individual-edge arc consistency imposes each scalar-image comparison separately and does not demand a globally compatible vector assignment. Blocked use: ['Combining parallel comparisons into a joint relation.', 'Using the consistency witness as a certificate of global constraint satisfiability.']
- **L-parameter** (hypothesis): The joint and separate conventions have different parameter shifts in the bijective-game construction. Blocked use: ['Using the same refinement dimension for both conventions without adjusting the game parameter.']
- **L-pure** (hypothesis): The pure-tuple disjoint-union comparison requires the stated dimension, connectedness, and diameter assumptions. Blocked use: ['Transferring pure-tuple histogram equivalence to arbitrary disconnected graph inputs or to dimensions outside the stated range.']
- **L-terminal** (hypothesis): The circuit simulation assumes an acyclic monotone circuit and terminal scalar test gates. Blocked use: ['Allowing a test gate to feed a downstream connection while retaining the stated false-test witness construction.', 'Applying the topological truth argument to cyclic circuits.']
- **L-slack** (hypothesis): The computation reduction requires strict time and spatial slack before it represents the line computation on a spatial ring. Blocked use: ['Using a ring that aliases the visited tape interval.', 'Ignoring the strict time cutoff needed to reach a frozen halting configuration.']
- **L-small** (edge_case): The smaller-parameter extension uses unrestricted graph inputs and matrix inspection; it does not extend the promised graph class to small dimensions. Blocked use: ['Claiming the diameter-restricted promised-class result for small dimensions from the matrix-inspection argument.']
- **L-refinement** (formal_scope): The lower bound concerns arbitrary deciders for the supplied-pair equivalence relation, rather than only the execution of refinement rounds. Blocked use: ['Treating a bound on refinement rounds as a substitute for this arbitrary-decider lower bound.', 'Transferring the result directly to graph identification, canonization, or isomorphism tasks.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 133 (`lean/docs/133.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Parity lifts and bounded-treewidth witnesses for Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence](../../preprints/Parity-lifts-and-bounded-treewidth-witnesses-for-Weisfeiler-Leman-equivalence-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [The complexity of identifying a graph by Weisfeiler–Leman refinement](../../preprints/The-complexity-of-identifying-a-graph-by-Weisfeiler-Leman-refinement-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Unconditional time lower bounds for Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence](../../preprints/Unconditional-time-lower-bounds-for-Weisfeiler-Leman-equivalence-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Variable-dimension Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence on general and subcubic graphs](../../preprints/Variable-dimension-Weisfeiler-Leman-equivalence-on-general-and-subcubic-graphs-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives the paper's parity-lift graph construction for every Weisfeiler–Leman dimension $k\ge4$, under both joint and separate conventions. From a finite choice system it constructs two equal-size uncolored graphs that are Weisfeiler–Leman equivalent exactly when the system has no successful compatible choice. The same equivalence holds for the pure variant.
>
> A successful choice is detected by the specified early round: round two in the joint convention and round one in the separate convention. The graph-size bound is linear in the choice-system size with a factor depending on $k$. The paper's conditional running-time exclusions are outside these selected construction statements.
>
> The formalization proves that deciding whether Weisfeiler–Leman refinement of a supplied dimension identifies a graph is EXPTIME-complete under polynomial-time many-one reductions. The input is a nonempty finite simple uncolored graph encoded by its adjacency matrix together with a positive binary-encoded dimension. Identification quantifies over every comparison graph, and the statement includes both the exponential-time upper bound and hardness.
>
> The formalization proves an unconditional deterministic time lower bound for deciding $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence. For every sufficiently large fixed $k$, every deciding algorithm in the stated multitape Turing-machine or sequential logarithmic-word RAM model requires at least $n^{ck}$ worst-case time for all sufficiently large graph orders $n$, for one absolute $c>0$.
>
> The bound covers both joint and separate replacement conventions and remains valid for explicit adjacency-matrix inputs restricted to simple connected uncolored graphs of diameter at most two.
>
> The formalization proves EXPTIME-completeness of deciding joint-update $k$-dimensional Weisfeiler–Leman equivalence when $k\ge2$ is part of the input in binary and the graphs are encoded by explicit adjacency matrices. It proves both the general result and the restriction to connected simple uncolored graphs of equal positive order and maximum degree at most three. Completeness uses polynomial-time many-one reductions.

Comparator source `WeisfeilerLeman.lean` (9150 bytes, sha256 `b541464a2729ed16e95cb44cbb15a4737a523c406b181d3e33f70dd53baadb69`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace WLTime

inductive Convention where
  | joint | separate
  deriving DecidableEq

def Graph (n : ℕ) :=
  { a : Fin n → Fin n → Bool //
    (∀ x y, a x y = a y x) ∧ (∀ x, a x x = false) }

namespace Graph

def adj {n : ℕ} (G : Graph n) : Fin n → Fin n → Bool := G.val

def diameterTwo {n : ℕ} (G : Graph n) : Prop :=
  0 < n ∧ ∀ x y, x = y ∨ G.adj x y = true ∨
    ∃ z, G.adj x z = true ∧ G.adj z y = true

noncomputable instance (n : ℕ) : Fintype (Graph n) := by
  classical
  unfold Graph
  infer_instance

end Graph

abbrev AtomicColor (k : ℕ) :=
  (Fin k → Fin k → Bool) × (Fin k → Fin k → Bool)

def Color (c : Convention) (k : ℕ) : ℕ → Type
  | 0 => AtomicColor k
  | t + 1 => match c with
    | .joint => Color c k t × Multiset (Fin k → Color c k t)
    | .separate => Color c k t × (Fin k → Multiset (Color c k t))

noncomputable def tupleColor {n : ℕ} (c : Convention) (k : ℕ) (G : Graph n) :
    (t : ℕ) → (Fin k → Fin n) → Color c k t
  | 0, v => (fun i j => decide (v i = v j), fun i j => G.adj (v i) (v j))
  | t + 1, v => match c with
    | .joint => (tupleColor .joint k G t v,
        Finset.univ.val.map (fun z => fun i =>
          tupleColor .joint k G t (Function.update v i z)))
    | .separate => (tupleColor .separate k G t v,
        fun i => Finset.univ.val.map (fun z =>
          tupleColor .separate k G t (Function.update v i z)))

noncomputable def histogram {n : ℕ} (c : Convention) (k : ℕ) (G : Graph n)
    (t : ℕ) : Multiset (Color c k t) :=
  Finset.univ.val.map (tupleColor c k G t)

def Equivalent {n : ℕ} (c : Convention) (k : ℕ) (G H : Graph n) : Prop :=
  ∀ t, histogram c k G t = histogram c k H t

def encodeNat (n : ℕ) : List Bool :=
  let bits := (Nat.digits 2 n).map (fun b => decide (b = 1))
  List.replicate bits.length true ++ [false] ++ bits

def matrixBits {n : ℕ} (G : Graph n) : List Bool :=
  (List.ofFn fun i : Fin n => List.ofFn fun j : Fin n => G.adj i j).flatten

def encodePair {n : ℕ} (G H : Graph n) : List Bool :=
  encodeNat n ++ matrixBits G ++ matrixBits H

inductive Move where
  | left | stay | right
  deriving DecidableEq

def Move.displacement : Move → ℤ
  | .left => -1
  | .stay => 0
  | .right => 1

structure TM where
  tapes : ℕ
  states : ℕ
  symbols : ℕ
  tapes_pos : 0 < tapes
  states_ge : 3 ≤ states
  symbols_ge : 3 ≤ symbols
  transition : Fin states → (Fin tapes → Fin symbols) →
    Fin states × (Fin tapes → Fin symbols) × (Fin tapes → Move)

namespace TM

structure Config (M : TM) where
  state : Fin M.states
  head : Fin M.tapes → ℤ
  tape : Fin M.tapes → ℤ → Fin M.symbols

def symbol (M : TM) (b : Bool) : Fin M.symbols :=
  if b then ⟨2, by have := M.symbols_ge; omega⟩ else
    ⟨1, by have := M.symbols_ge; omega⟩

def initial (M : TM) (w : List Bool) : M.Config where
  state := ⟨0, by have := M.states_ge; omega⟩
  head := fun _ => 0
  tape := fun a z => if a.val = 0 ∧ 0 ≤ z ∧ z.toNat < w.length then
    M.symbol (w.getD z.toNat false) else ⟨0, by have := M.symbols_ge; omega⟩

def halted (M : TM) (C : M.Config) : Prop := C.state.val = 1 ∨ C.state.val = 2

instance (M : TM) (C : M.Config) : Decidable (M.halted C) := inferInstanceAs
  (Decidable (C.state.val = 1 ∨ C.state.val = 2))

def step (M : TM) (C : M.Config) : M.Config :=
  if M.halted C then C else
  let tr := M.transition C.state (fun a => C.tape a (C.head a))
  { state := tr.1
    head := fun a => C.head a + (tr.2.2 a).displacement
    tape := fun a z => if z = C.head a then tr.2.1 a else C.tape a z }

def run (M : TM) (w : List Bool) (t : ℕ) : M.Config := M.step^[t] (M.initial w)

def halts (M : TM) (w : List Bool) : Prop := ∃ t, M.halted (M.run w t)

noncomputable def time (M : TM) (w : List Bool) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact if h : M.halts w then Nat.find h else 0

def accepts (M : TM) (w : List Bool) : Prop :=
  ∃ t, (M.run w t).state.val = 1

def outputs (M : TM) (input output : List Bool) : Prop :=
  M.halts input ∧ ∀ i : Fin output.length,
    (M.run input (M.time input)).tape ⟨0, M.tapes_pos⟩ (i.val : ℤ) =
      M.symbol (output.get i)

end TM

abbrev Word (w : ℕ) := Fin (2 ^ w)

def wordBits {w : ℕ} (a : Word w) : List Bool :=
  List.ofFn (fun i : Fin w => a.val.testBit i.val)

def registerBits {R w : ℕ} (a : Fin R → Word w) : List Bool :=
  (List.ofFn fun i : Fin R => wordBits (a i)).flatten

structure WordOperation (R : ℕ) where
  apply : (w : ℕ) → (Fin R → Word w) → (Fin R → Word w)
  implementation : TM
  exponent : ℕ
  constant : ℕ
  computes : ∀ w a, implementation.outputs
    (encodeNat w ++ registerBits a) (registerBits (apply w a))
  cost : ∀ w (a : Fin R → Word w), implementation.time (encodeNat w ++ registerBits a) ≤
    constant * (w + 1) ^ exponent

inductive RAMInstruction (R P : ℕ) where
  | halt (answer : Bool)
  | jump (pc : Fin P)
  | compute (op : WordOperation R) (next : RAMInstruction R P)
  | load (dest addr : Fin R) (next : RAMInstruction R P)
  | store (addr src : Fin R) (next : RAMInstruction R P)
  | branchZero (reg : Fin R) (yes no : RAMInstruction R P)

structure WordWidth where
  bits : ℕ → ℕ
  positive : ∀ n, 0 < bits n
  bound : ℕ
  logarithmic : ∀ n, bits n ≤ bound * (Nat.log2 (n + 2) + 1)

structure RAM where
  registers : ℕ
  locations : ℕ
  locations_pos : 0 < locations
  width : WordWidth
  program : Fin locations → RAMInstruction registers locations
  input_fits : ∀ n (G H : Graph n),
    (encodePair G H).length ≤ width.bits n * 2 ^ width.bits n

namespace RAM

structure Config (A : RAM) (n : ℕ) where
  pc : Fin A.locations
  registers : Fin A.registers → Word (A.width.bits n)
  memory : Word (A.width.bits n) → Word (A.width.bits n)
  answer : Option Bool

def packedWord (w : ℕ) (input : List Bool) (a : Word w) : Word w :=
  ⟨(∑ i ∈ Finset.range w, if input.getD (w * a.val + i) false then 2 ^ i else 0)
      % (2 ^ w), Nat.mod_lt _ (by positivity)⟩

def initial (A : RAM) (n : ℕ) (w : List Bool) : A.Config n where
  pc := ⟨0, A.locations_pos⟩
  registers := fun _ => ⟨0, by positivity⟩
  memory := packedWord (A.width.bits n) w
  answer := none

def execute (A : RAM) {n : ℕ} :
    RAMInstruction A.registers A.locations → A.Config n → A.Config n
  | .halt b, C => { C with answer := some b }
  | .jump p, C => { C with pc := p }
  | .compute op next, C => A.execute next { C with
      registers := op.apply (A.width.bits n) C.registers }
  | .load dest addr next, C => A.execute next { C with
      registers := Function.update C.registers dest (C.memory (C.registers addr)) }
  | .store addr src next, C => A.execute next { C with
      memory := Function.update C.memory (C.registers addr) (C.registers src) }
  | .branchZero reg yes no, C =>
      if (C.registers reg).val = 0 then A.execute yes C else A.execute no C

def step (A : RAM) {n : ℕ} (C : A.Config n) : A.Config n :=
  match C.answer with
  | some _ => C
  | none => A.execute (A.program C.pc) C

def run (A : RAM) (n : ℕ) (w : List Bool) (t : ℕ) : A.Config n :=
  A.step^[t] (A.initial n w)

def halts (A : RAM) (n : ℕ) (w : List Bool) : Prop :=
  ∃ t, (A.run n w t).answer.isSome = true

noncomputable def time (A : RAM) (n : ℕ) (w : List Bool) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact if h : A.halts n w then Nat.find h else 0

def accepts (A : RAM) (n : ℕ) (w : List Bool) : Prop :=
  ∃ t, (A.run n w t).answer = some true

end RAM

inductive Model where
  | turing (machine : TM)
  | ram (machine : RAM)

def Model.halts (A : Model) (n : ℕ) (w : List Bool) : Prop := match A with
  | .turing M => M.halts w
  | .ram M => M.halts n w

def Model.accepts (A : Model) (n : ℕ) (w : List Bool) : Prop := match A with
  | .turing M => M.accepts w
  | .ram M => M.accepts n w

noncomputable def Model.time (A : Model) (n : ℕ) (w : List Bool) : ℕ := match A with
  | .turing M => M.time w
  | .ram M => M.time n w

inductive InputClass where
  | unrestricted | diameterTwo
  deriving DecidableEq

def InputClass.contains (p : InputClass) {n : ℕ} (G H : Graph n) : Prop := match p with
  | .unrestricted => True
  | .diameterTwo => G.diameterTwo ∧ H.diameterTwo

def Decides (A : Model) (c : Convention) (k : ℕ) (p : InputClass) : Prop :=
  ∀ n (G H : Graph n), p.contains G H →
    A.halts n (encodePair G H) ∧
      (A.accepts n (encodePair G H) ↔ Equivalent c k G H)

noncomputable def worstTime (A : Model) (p : InputClass) (n : ℕ) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact Finset.univ.sup (fun pair : Graph n × Graph n =>
    if p.contains pair.1 pair.2 then A.time n (encodePair pair.1 pair.2) else 0)

def MainClaim : Prop :=
  ∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ ∃ k₀ : ℕ, ∀ k : ℕ, k₀ ≤ k →
    ∀ convention : Convention, ∀ p : InputClass, ∀ A : Model,
      Decides A convention k p → ∃ n₀ : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, n₀ ≤ n →
        (n : ℝ) ^ (c * (k : ℝ)) ≤ (worstTime A p n : ℝ)

end WLTime

theorem WLTime.unconditional_time_lower_bound : WLTime.MainClaim := by
  sorry

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every sufficiently large fixed <i>k</i>, deciding whether two <i>n</i>-vertex graphs are <i>k</i>-Weisfeiler–Leman equivalent requires $`n^{\Omega(k)}`$ deterministic sequential time in the worst case. The bound holds at every sufficiently large graph order, even for simple connected uncolored graphs of diameter at most two, without a complexity assumption. Inputs are explicit adjacency matrices; the models are multitape Turing machines and sequential logarithmic-word RAMs with fixed polynomial-bit-time instructions. Both joint and separate replacement conventions are covered.
